### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: ASE disclosed a strong set of quarterly results and guidance 
on 29 October, both beating market expectations, thanks to the strong AI 
scaling which is just getting stronger to benefit ASE’s leading-edge 
advanced packaging (LEAP) business. We raise our forecasts and 12M TP 
and reaffirm our Outperform (2) call on the stock; accumulate into any 
technical pullback.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. ASE reported 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD27bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 25%/18% 
thanks to higher semiconductor assembly & test (SAT, or IC ATM by 
company definition) revenue which came in at TWD151bn (USD4.7bn), 7% 
above our estimate, for higher gross margin (GM) of 30.5%, 1.9pp above 
ours and topping ASE’s structural target of 24-30%. The strength was 
amplified further by lower non-operating losses, resulting in the strong 
bottom-line beat (table on p.2). Guidance was another beat thanks to the 
strong SAT business where ASE expects revenue to grow 11-13% QoQ for 
GM to top its record of 30.5% during the COVID upturn. We raise our 2026-
28E EPS by 14-24% assuming its SAT GM to reach 33% through 2027.

Strong AI gets stronger. Discussions centred on ASE’s LEAP business, 
where management further hiked revenue target this year beyond USD4bn 
(USD1.6bn in 2025), after 3 hikes YTD, thanks to strong demand for its on-
substrate (oS) packaging and initial ramp of its full-process solutions. With 
addition of new co-packaged optics (CPO) solutions, ASE expects the 
LEAP revenue to double YoY in 2027. As LEAP is margin-accretive, ASE 
expects the SAT GM to continue improving beyond 4Q26, making the 
ceiling of its structural GM target now as the floor to kick off 2027 for a 
strong start. Given management’s emphasis on “friendly pricing”, we expect 
likely continued cost passthrough across the supply chain to add to GM 
upside. Working hard to narrow the supply/demand gap, ASE further raised 
this year’s capex to USD11.5bn (from USD10.5bn), reinforcing our view 
that AI is scaling strongly to accelerate the chip industry growth.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for ASE to TWD710 (previous: TWD550), based on our revised ROE-
adjusted PBR target of 5.8x (previous: 5.2x) 2027E BVPS due to a higher 
ROE trajectory. Our revised TP translates into 24x 4-quarter forward PER, 
which we see as justifying the rich PBR target given its 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 25%. We reiterate our Outperform (2) call with key downside risks 
of any “silicon inflation” and geopolitical overhang leading to AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 5-10% above the consensus as we 
believe the street has yet to factor in its stronger LEAP business.

29 October 2026

Entering an even stronger earnings cycle (III)

➢ 3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance all beat expectations 
➢ Strong AI scaling is getting even stronger to benefit LEAP 
➢ Reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD710

Company profile 
Established in April 2018, ASE Industrial Holding Co (HoldCo) is a holding company which privatized 
ASE and SPIL fully on 30 April 2018 through equity and cash swap, respectively, and listed on the same 
day with senior management assigned mainly by ASE. HoldCo has become the strong No.1 OSAT 
maker in the world, offering a comprehensive product portfolio to its customers from wirebonding, flip-
chip & bumping, system-in-package (SiP) to broad-based electronics manufacturing services (EMS).

### Page 5

5

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
ASE’s Board of Directors consists of nine members with diverse professional backgrounds.
Three of the directors are independent, representing 33 percent of the board. The Chairman also
serves as the Chief Executive Officer, resulting in unified leadership and centralized decision-
making. To maintain effective governance and oversight under this structure, ASE relies on its
independent directors and established board processes. The board currently includes one
female director, and the company continues to pursue long-term goals related to board diversity
and balanced representation. ASE has an Audit Committee, Compensation Committee, Risk
Management Committee, and Chief Government Officer to conduct effective supervision.
Capital management 3
In the past 5 years, ASE paid over 50% or nearly 50% of its earnings in dividends. However, the
dividend trend was volatile in dollar and payout ratio terms. We forecast its dividend payout ratio
to average over 50% in the next 2 years.
Related party &
transaction 2
ASE undertakes related-party transactions with its subsidiaries on sales and purchases. The
total related-party transaction sales/purchase amounts were less than 10% of its total
sales/purchases, and the transaction terms with related parties were normal compared with
those with other customers/suppliers. Thus, we do not think related-party transactions have a
meaningful impact on overall operations.
S Supply chain management 2
The supply of raw materials has the most direct impact on ASE's operations and manufacturing.
To lower overall supply-chain risks, ASE has expanded the scope of sustainability risk
management to non-tier-1 suppliers. ASE currently has over 600 non-tier-1 suppliers.
ASE requires its suppliers to comply with its Supplier Code of Conduct, which aims to ensure
that suppliers conduct business in a manner that meets labour, health & safety, environment,
business ethics & management, and various corporate compliance standards.
E Water & wastewater management 2
ASE has adopted 3 water-use strategies of reduce, reuse and recycle. Through its water
management measures, it reduced water consumption density (water consumption/revenue) by
43% in 2024 vs. that of 2015. ASE is committed to increasing water reuse at its facilities in order
to improve water efficiency.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
ASE is committed to improving waste recycling and reuse through the 5 key elements of a
circular economy of: direct recycling, reuse, off-site regeneration, renewable materials, and
supply and rental. The recycling rate of general waste was 90% in 2024 and its hazardous waste
density declined by 53% in 2024 vs. that of 2015.
E GHG emissions 1
ASE is committed to achieving net zero emissions by advancing low carbon energy adoption,
developing smart grid technologies, and strengthening high tech energy management systems.
As of 2024, 19% of total manufacturing energy consumption comes from renewable sources,
88% of ASE facilities use renewable energy, and 10 sites have achieved RE100 certification.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

3-month preview: Landmark’s Sep revenues were up 190% YoY/ 4% MoM to 
NT$540m, supported by strong demand on 800G / 1.6T SiPh optical modules and 
better InP substrate supply. Looking ahead, we expect company’s Oct/ Nov/ Dec 
revenues to see sequential MoM growth at 5%/ 5%/ 8%, resulting in 4Q revenues 
growth at 16% QoQ to NT$1.8bn, with company’s continuous capacity expansion.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 result and revise up earnings by 9%/ 
3%/ 2%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of SiPh CW lasers, supported by 
strong client demand and capacity expansion. We revise up GM by 1ppts in 2026E on 
mix upgrade, and revise down Opex ratio in 2026-29E to reflect improving operational 
efficiency amid the larger scale.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,490 (vs. previously at NT$4,459). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
75x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,490. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI fell to 49.3 in October from 50.1 in September:

• This came in lower than both our estimate and consensus of 49.9.
• The production index decreased 1.9ppt while the new order index declined
1.1ppt MoM.
Our view:
• We believe the soft manufacturing PMI data could weigh on market
sentiment in the near term. Nonetheless, we prefer supply players with
company-specific drivers such as Airtac and Hiwin:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 16x 2027e P/E, vs. the 25x average since 2020 (see our
30 Oct 2026 report).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 28x 2027e P/E, vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

Grand Process Technology Corp. (3131.TWO): SoIC / PLP to support 
future GM enhancement; 3Q26 GM miss
3 November 2026 | 10:54PM HKT
GPTC reported its 3Q26 preliminary results. Revenues was pre-announced, while GM 
came in at 36.6% (down 2.3ppt QoQ; down 4.2ppt YoY), below GSe/cons. of 
39.7%/40.6%, which we attribute to an unfavorable product mix. Opex ratio further 
improved to 14.6% (vs. 17.7% in 2Q26), better than our / Bloomberg consensus 
estimates of 15.1% / 16.3%, leading OP income to +34% QoQ, or +54% YoY. Overall, 
the miss on GM leads EPS miss our / Bloomberg consensus by 9% / 14%. Despite 
3Q26 GM miss, we believe GPTC’s multi-year growth outlook remains intact, with 
continuous capacity increase to better capture the demand from CoWoS capacity 
expansion, along with product mix upgrade toward SoIC and PLP , carrying higher 
content value. Maintain Buy.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut EPS by 4% / 2% / 0% in 
2026-28E, mainly on lower GM, as we slowdown the pace of product mix upgrade 
toward SoIC and PLP . Despite the cut, we continue to expect GM sequential 
enhancement, with 4Q26 GM up to 37.7% (vs. 36.6% in 3Q26) and up to 40.2% / 
42.5% in 2027-28E, driven by rising SoIC and PLP contribution.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 210: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명SK하이닉스(000660) HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
90 
SK하이닉스  (000660) 
2026.11.04

HBM 하나로 시가총액 설명 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

업황 우려에 고유 할인까지 겹친 과도한 하락 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 230만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27 년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다[표1]. 이는 상장 이후 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 해당한다. 
‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 102%, 66%, 47%로, ‘26년 정점 이후에도 과거 업사이클 
고점보다 높은 수준이 유지될 전망이다.  
한편, 이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해져 시장의 시야가 ‘28년으로 향할 경우 
‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 360만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 57% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -51%를 넘어섰고, 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
그러나 당사는 아래와 같은 투자포인트와 향후 주가 흐름을 제시한다.

‘28년 HBM 가치, 시가총액의 1.3배 
HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 cHBM으로 
갈수록 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 
물량과 가격이 결정된다. 동사는 이 전환이 업계에서 가장 선두권이다.  
당사는 동사 HBM 매출이 ‘26년 43조원에서 ‘28년 183조원으로 증가할 것으로 
전망한다. 만약, 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E(2 1배, 
15배, 1 2배)를 적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 47%, 93%, 135%를 
설명한다[표3]. ‘27 년에 이미 HBM 하나만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명된다는 
의미다. 시장이 통상적으로 받아들이는 멀티플 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 
다르지 않다.

150만원에 사면 ‘30년 EPS 2배 
동사의 주주환원은 자사주 매입/소각 중심일 것으로 상정한다. 당사는 동사가 현 
정책 기간(‘25~’27년)에 이어 ‘28년 이후에도 FCF의 50%를 환원하고 이 중 
70%를 자사주 매입/소각에 쓸 것으로 가정했다.  
당사 가정대로라면 총주주환원수익률은 현 시가총액 대비 ‘27년 14%, ‘28~’30년 
연 17~24%다[표4]. 
관건은 매입 단가다. 자사주 매입의 주당가치 개선 효과는 P/E가 낮을수록 
커지는데, 현 주가의 ‘27년 P/E는 2.7배다. 현 주가 수준인 150만원에 매년 균등 
매입한다고 ‘30년까지 주식수는 50% 줄어든다.   
그 결과 ‘30년 주당순이익은 매입이 없을 때 대비 2배가 된다[표4]. ‘28년 이후 
이익이 정체되더라도 주당 이익은 계속 늘어나는 구조이며, 이익 지속성에 대한 
의심이 주가를 누를수록 소각 효과는 커진다. 현 주가 수준의 정체가 장기화되기 
어려운 이유다.

Buy (Initiation)

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간이다. 솔리다임 상장 철회에 이어 3분기 
실적발표에서 ‘26년 주주환원 100조원과 매 분기 자사주 매입 방침이 제시되면서 환원 
의지는 검증되었다. 
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 불확실성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 가격 하락폭은 LTA 
방어력이 확인되기 전까지 블랙박스다. 이 불확실성이 멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 
계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 구간이 이어질 전망이다. 이 구간의 
기회비용은 환원으로 보상받는다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 신규 팹 물량이 소량 
출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 당사는 2H27에 ‘28년 HBM 협의와 
LTA 연장이 지속적으로 확인되면서 이익이 스트레스 테스트 범위 안에서 유지될 것으로 
전망하며[그림2], 이것이 숫자로 확인되는 시점에 순환 할인이 축소되고 재평가가 나타날 
것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.

## Document 214: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.  
목표주가 (유지) 4,000,000원

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

 절대 (달러환산) (15.2) (10.5) 21.4

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

的股票。瑞银数据显⽰，周四抛压主要来⾃对冲基⾦。以摩根⼤通数据估算，散⼾净卖出
约 13 亿美元，单⽇规模创⼀年新⾼。 “ 恐慌指数 ”VIX 盘中飙升⾄⼋个⽉⾼位。投资者抢购保

摘要：
美东时间 11 ⽉ 5 ⽇周四，美股科技股遭遇猛烈抛售之际，⾼盛集团的股票交易台电话⼏乎被 “ 打
爆 ” ，投资者纷纷打来询问市场还可能跌多深，但真正愿意逢低买⼊的资⾦却寥寥⽆⼏。
⾼盛交易员 John Flood 在发给客⼾的市场评论中形容，周四的交易环境与前⼀⽇截然相反：周
三是该⾏股票交易台全年最繁忙的交易⽇之⼀，⽽周四则 “ 完全相反 ” ，交易员接到⼤量询问市场
情况的电话，却⼏乎看不到买盘进场。
这番描述折射出 AI 交易突然降温后，市场情绪从追涨迅速转向避险。纳斯达克综合指数周四收跌
5.52% ，费城半导体指数暴跌超过 12% ，科技股整体市值蒸发约 2.5 万亿美元。与此同时，波动

率飙升、散⼾集中卖出、对冲基⾦削减仓位，进⼀步放⼤了市场跌势。
⾼盛交易员眼中的市场反转：周三积极买⼊，周四急于撤退
Flood 表⽰，周三市场上涨后，部分此前在美国中期选举前⼤幅降低⻛险敞⼝的投资者重新⼊
场，买回了⼀些股票。但周四市场突然逆转，部分对冲基⾦⼜开始卖出前⼀⽇刚刚买⼊的股
票，尤其是⼤型科技股和超⼤市值科技公司。
他指出，⻓期投资机构再度陷⼊观望，市场尚未出现愿意出⼿托底的买家。与此同时，⾯向散
⼾客⼾的券商也反映，卖盘连续第⼆天明显多于买盘。
交易活跃度同样出现异常。 Flood 称，截⾄周四盘中，标普 500 指数 ETF SPY 成交量较过去 20 个
交易⽇均值⾼出约 160% ，追踪纳斯达克 100 指数的 QQQ 成交量⾼出约 100% ，半导体 ETF SMH
成交量⾼出约 150% ， DRAM 相关股票成交量则⾼出约 75% 。
成交量激增反映出投资者正在密集调整仓位，但并不意味着所有交易都是净卖出。结合交易员
所描述的买盘缺位，市场更明显的信号是：在股价快速下跌时，原本可能⼊场承接的资⾦选择

了等待。
机构抛压集中释放，散⼾单⽇净卖出规模创近⼀年新⾼
⾼盛交易台观察到的买盘退缩，也得到了其他机构交易数据的印证。
据路透社报道，瑞银交易员数据显⽰，周四该⾏机构客⼾的主动交易流向明显偏向卖出，抛压
主要来⾃对冲基⾦，科技股尤其是存储芯⽚和半导体板块遭遇集中抛售。瑞银交易员将新兴云
服务商和半导体股票的⾛势形容为明显的 “ 投降式抛售 ” 。
⾼盛交易员也指出，周四对冲基⾦正在卖出前⼀⽇重新买⼊的部分⼤型科技股。市场不仅出现
板块内部轮动，更有投资者直接削减整体⻛险敞⼝。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

散⼾投资者同样加⼊撤退⾏列。 Vanda Research 的数据显⽰，周四散⼾投资者单只股票净卖出
规模有望创下疫情以来最⼤单⽇纪录。摩根⼤通截⾄美东时间上午 11 时 30 分的数据则显⽰，若
将 ETF 计⼊，散⼾净卖出规模达到约 13 亿美元，为过去⼀年最⾼。
更值得注意的是，散⼾通常会在市场下跌时买⼊股票 “ 抄底 ” ，但这⼀次，个股市场的逢低买盘明
显减弱。 Vanda Research 策略师指出，传统的下跌⾏情中散⼾往往会买⼊回调，但周四这种买
盘实际上已经从个股中消失。
VIX 盘中飙升⾄⼋个⽉⾼位，投资者抢购保护性期权
股票抛售之外，投资者对冲市场⻛险的需求也迅速升温。
衡量市场波动的 “ 恐慌指数 ”—— 芝加哥期权交易所波动率指数（ VIX ）周四盘中⼀度上涨 11.75
点，升⾄ 28.36 ，为⼋个⽉⾼位，创 2025 年 4 ⽉美国对等关税冲击以来最⼤单⽇盘中涨幅；收盘
报 28.18 。
期权市场的变化同样显⽰，投资者正在为进⼀步下跌购买保护。路透社援引期权分析机构

Mercury Alerts 的数据称，周四主要股指产品的看跌期权权利⾦约为通常⽔平的两倍，反映出市
场对下⾏保护的需求显著增加。
互动券商⾸席策略师 Steve Sosnick 表⽰，市场情绪在短短⼀天内发⽣了明显变化：周三投资者
还认为没有必要购买保护，周四却突然意识到需要对冲⻛险。
此外，杠杆和反向 ETF 以及部分量化策略通常会在临近收盘时根据市场波动调整仓位，这可能进
⼀步放⼤尾盘涨跌。野村证券盘中估算，杠杆 ETF 周四收盘前可能需要卖出约 470 亿美元股票，
这将是该机构⾃ 2006 年开始追踪相关数据以来第⼆⼤规模的此类卖盘，且卖出压⼒主要集中在
科技和 AI 相关基⾦。
这意味着，部分卖盘并⾮完全来⾃投资者主动改变⻓期判断，也可能是市场下跌触发了策略性
减仓，进⽽形成进⼀步的下跌压⼒。
数据中⼼建设与 AI 投资回报遭质疑，抛售从芯⽚蔓延⾄产业链
本轮抛售的直接背景，是 AI 相关股票此前累积了⼤量资⾦和较⾼市场预期，⽽美国中期选举结果
⼜令数据中⼼建设前景蒙上政策阴影。

⽬采取更严格的审批、能源和环保要求，从⽽拖慢美国 AI 基础设施建设。半导体、存储芯⽚、新
兴云服务商以及数据中⼼电⼒供应商因此受到冲击。
⾼盛交易员 Flood 还提出了⼀个更具体的担忧：到 2028 年，市场可能⾯临数据中⼼⼟地、电⼒和
建筑容量不⾜的问题。如果数据中⼼基础设施建设速度跟不上芯⽚交付，市场就可能从担忧芯
⽚供应不⾜，转向担忧芯⽚过剩、缺少⾜够的数据中⼼承接。
这也解释了为何周四的抛售并不局限于芯⽚制造商。数据中⼼建设承包商、电⼒供应商、新兴
云服务商等板块也遭遇明显卖压。投资者开始重新审视 AI 产业链的扩张节奏，以及巨额算⼒投资
能否按预期转化为实际收⼊和利润。
与此同时，近期⾼通和 Arm 的业绩展望未能满⾜市场预期，也进⼀步削弱了投资者对半导体板块
的信⼼。 AI 需求⻓期增⻓的逻辑并未因此消失，但市场对增⻓兑现速度、资本开⽀回报以及产业
链各环节盈利能⼒的要求正在提⾼。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

对于市场⽽⾔，关键问题已经从 “AI 投资还会增⻓多少 ” ，转向 “ 这些投资能否按时建成、获得⾜
够电⼒，并产⽣⾜以⽀撑当前估值的回报 ” 。当投资者同时选择减仓、对冲和等待买⼊机会时，
交易员电话被打爆，却看不到买家进场，也就成为这轮 AI 抛售中最直观的市场情绪写照。

## Document 412: Rates Spark_ Treasury heat from tech flight - but for how long_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ Treasury heat from tech flight - but for how long_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

A creeping move lower in equities would eventually be ignored
by Treasuries. But a large dump in equities would not. Treasuries
are so far very much in vogue. In Europe, bond spreads are
widening again as we head into another weekend of street
unrest. Friday sees US jobs data competing for the spotlight,
although Thursday's low claims were broadly ignored

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Benjamin Schroeder and Michiel Tukker
AI jitters in equity markets has led to the UST 10yr yield dropping by about
50bp from recent highs
A creeping move lower in equities would
eventually be ignored by Treasuries. But a
large dump in equities would not
The US 10yr yield is now down some 50bp from its recent highs.
The drivers have been real money value hunters (mostly domestic,
but also including international players), some fast money longs in
Treasuries versus payers in SOFR in a short swap spread trade, and
ﬁnally a risk-oﬀ tone in the AI space that's been counteracted by
safety ﬂows into Treasuries. The latter has been the dominant

### Page 2

driver of the last 10-15bp, which has taken the 10yr yield down to
the low 4.80s%. Should the AI rout intensify, it's tough to see long-
end yields doing anything other than trekking still lower in
sympathy.
To what extent is this a US election-driven outcome? We could paint
a narrative in which electorate support for fewer data centres and
greater scrutiny of the AI space is a factor. To an extent, it is. But the
bigger picture sees AI valuations generally under pressure. How

long this persists is tough to call, but suﬃce it to say that the
technical picture does not look great for the coming few weeks at
least. The 4.65% area for the 10yr yield is where we could settle
should the risk-oﬀ tone intensify.
That said, the market has conveniently ignored the elevated
inﬂation environment, the largesse in ﬁscal deﬁcits and the AI
growth boost, which remains ongoing. Thursday saw another very
low jobless claims outcome, and Friday is liable to see the

unemployment rate reconﬁrmed at 4.2% (very low). At some point,
Treasuries will refocus on fundamentals. A creeping move lower in
equities would eventually be ignored by Treasuries. But a large
dump in equities would not; that's where the fall in yields becomes
an even larger lurch lower. We're not calling for it, but we're
watching carefully.
AI jitters take over
The bullish momentum in rates continued, but the driving force
behind it changed. While Thursday’s early session was still marked

by an extension of post-election positive risk sentiment, sentiment
later soured on AI jitters. The VIX index – the equity market’s fear
gauge – jumped to the highest since March as tech stocks came
under increasing pressure.
In bond markets, souring sentiment made itself felt in rewidening
spreads. The 10y OAT/Bund spread ended its brief recovery below
150bp. To be fair, the market also found itself confronted with more
headlines around protests in France, highlighting the political

challenge the country faces trying to consolidate its ﬁnances.

Markets will be looking to the US jobs data to inform their Fed view.
However, the data thus so has kept us well within the low-hire / low-
ﬁre narrative, so it would probably take a larger surprise to impact
the market and take the eyes oﬀ the renewed AI concerns.
Bank of England opens door to hawkish
repricing
The Bank of England hiked as expected, but revised inﬂation
forecasts add additional upside risk to rates. Markets now price in a
50% chance that the next hike will be in December, and then at

least one more hike later in 2027. Every committee member agreed
to hike rates, which means even the doves are on board. This lowers
the bar for future hikes and could add to an already volatile rates
environment. Any upside inﬂation surprises or another surge in oil
or gas prices could quickly turn the narrative more hawkish.
Having said that, the sterling curve still seems too steep to us,
especially when compared to euro and dollar curves. The price

action today also shows that longer-dated rates struggled to move
higher – rates up to 2Y rose, but the 10Y swap rate ended lower on
the day. Part of this suggests markets are front-loading more hikes,
but are turning more sceptical about the BoE's terminal rate. We,
too, struggle to see the BoE end the hiking cycle around 4.5%.

## Document 431: The nowcasts, updated scenario and forecasts of the Economic Research - 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The nowcasts, updated scenario and forecasts of the Economic Research -  2 November 2026 (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

economic
-
research.bnpparibas.com

Check out the economic research nowcasts on November 9. 
In the meantime, you can read our analysis of the third
-
quarter growth figures 
here

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.6% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of German defence spending on the real economy . Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we anticipate another 
25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That said, strong er than 
expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q  in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aero nautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before  rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 14 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 15 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.
